## Description

<!-- SECTION:DESCRIPTION:BEGIN -->
Implement the user's Niks3 contract reviewed against MR !329. Niks3 has separate write control and Nix read planes. Private agent cache reads currently lack credential transport. User authorized implementation from origin/dev and an MR after verification; preserve untracked dev/session-ses_f927.md. Initial scope is static-token or mTLS writes and public or mTLS reads. External providers and Bearer/OIDC reads are deferred. Do not refactor generic push_to naming or cache priorities.
<!-- SECTION:DESCRIPTION:END -->

## Acceptance Criteria
<!-- AC:BEGIN -->
- [x] #1 Niks3 is a distinct validated cache type across configuration and protocol; unknown types fail closed
- [x] #2 Separate write/API URL and read/substituter URL with multiple signing keys are persisted using a new migration
- [x] #3 Tokens and private keys are encrypted and redacted; configured flags and atomic credential replacement and clearing are supported
- [x] #4 Remote builder and local cache worker push through packaged Niks3 CLI with protected temporary files and no secrets in argv or logs
- [ ] #5 Builder secrets and agent read secrets require verified confidential transport; agents receive only environment-scoped read credentials
- [ ] #6 Server independently verifies publication using the selected destination identity and authenticated Nix read configuration
- [ ] #7 Agent deployment and CVE materialization support public and mTLS reads with signature verification and credential cleanup
- [ ] #8 Discovery and structured non-mutating connection tests enforce existing SSRF and redirect policy
- [ ] #9 Design and production UI support discovery and separate read/write authentication for create and edit with loading and failure states
- [ ] #10 Focused unit tests and Niks3 VM tests cover remote builder plus agent public/private flows environment isolation CVE materialization and secret-log audits
- [ ] #11 Packaging preserves evaluator Nix selection; server and builder packages plus Niks3 integration and focused authoritative Web UI gates pass; full-suite and legacy-cache regression outcomes are recorded under the user's explicit review exceptions
- [ ] #12 Add cache opens one coherent design-style modal immediately for S3-compatible Attic Nix HTTPS and Niks3; switching types retains common and applicable type-specific drafts in the same shell
- [ ] #13 Authoritative browser workflows create representative configurations for all four types through the real fixture API and preserve all Niks3 security/scope regressions; inspected section-by-section and narrow screenshots prove accepted design parity
<!-- AC:END -->

## Implementation Plan

<!-- SECTION:PLAN:BEGIN -->
1. Preserve origin/dev baseline in dedicated TASK-470 worktree. Use current cf-config re-export rather than obsolete server config copies.
2. Add additive protocol types CacheReadAuth (None/Mtls) and Niks3WriteAuth (Token/Mtls), redacted Debug and defaults. Add shared cf-config credential preparation owning protected temporary files through child completion; share it for push commands and Nix read URL/CA/key preparation.
3. Add next-free migration and Niks3 model/DTO fields. Extend existing encryption/decryption/update layer with explicit clear flags and atomic mode-transition cleanup. API responses redact all private material and expose configured flags.
4. Wire builder and server local pushes to one shared Niks3 command preparation; avoid AWS credentials and multiplied per-job concurrency. Extend destination ID completion and confidentiality checks; preserve server-side Nix readability verification.
5. Wire agent heartbeat read-only auth with verified transport checks, deployment pull, and CVE materialization to the shared read helper.
6. Add protected discovery and non-mutating structured connectivity checks; disable redirects or validate each hop using existing target policy.
7. Start task-owned devenv preview with isolated DB and mock fixtures before UI changes. Update design plus create/edit/discovery/auth UI and behavior tests.
8. Package CLI preserving evaluator Nix PATH; add focused Niks3 VM fixture/check covering all required flows and regressions. Prepare SQLx only against verified task DB. Review documentation and raw verification results before commit/push/MR.
Initial static-token and mTLS scope is approved. External providers and Bearer/OIDC reads are not implemented in this task.

Focused local-worker corrections implemented only in packages/default/crates/cf-server/src/builder/cache_worker.rs. Inspected CachePushJob: identity is cache_destination (persisted push_to URL reference) plus derivation_id; no DB destination ID or environment field exists. Startup no longer chooses or snapshots the first enabled DB destination. Every normal/batch queued attempt resolves derivation environment with remote active-system/commit-flake/configuration-name precedence, rejects same-precedence cross-environment ambiguity, checks unique current reference across all DB rows (including disabled rows), enabled status and remote-style assigned-cache/global fallback eligibility, then maps current decrypted credentials. Resolution failures record generic credential-safe job failures and cannot select another destination. Static-only legacy jobs retain null/matching-reference support; static Niks3 requires an explicit reference. Unscoped automatic job creation runs only with no DB destination rows and rechecks that condition each tick. Batch attempts use process_one_job and queue retry/backoff rather than stale push_to_cache_with_retry configuration. A process-owned global Niks3 slot serializes both entry points; waiting attempts refresh again after acquiring it, and cancellation of the waiter retains the slot until child completion. Existing unrelated/shared work preserved; no query/model/handler/build_jobs/schema files edited by this correction owner; no services or commits.

The canonical helper also recovers recorded successful remote dispatch ID from build_jobs before selecting a new destination; supplied differing IDs fail closed. Second local-publication correction final owned files: packages/default/crates/cf-server/src/builder/cache_worker.rs; src/builder/worker.rs; src/queries/cache_push.rs (CachePushJob plus queries); new migrations/0271_cache_push_destination_identity.sql.0271 adds nullable INTEGER cache_destination_id with NO FK and NOT NULL source default legacy plus provenance CHECK. No inferred backfill: existing legacy names/URLs resolve only to one current enabled eligible DB destination and are pinned to its ID before pushing. Unknown/NULL legacy references and legacy rows with no matching DB destination fail closed, including old static-looking URLs, because deleted-DB provenance cannot be disproved. Only explicit static rows use matching enabled static config. Database rows retain ID after deletion; deleted/ineligible IDs never resolve via names/URLs or static settings. New local build enqueue runs after authoritative output persistence; DB-only Niks3 requires no static push_to. Missing-job recovery uses the same canonical resolver and respects successful remote dispatch IDs. Queue insertion locks derivation then existing rows; differing provenance/ID cannot retarget any existing publication, and matching jobs retain status/backoff. Conditional attempt claims prevent two workers from overwriting the same active/completed job. Normal and batch execution use the shared current destination resolver, with the existing process-owned global Niks3 slot and refresh after waiting. No priorities, handlers, build_jobs/model/schema0269/0270 or credential models edited by this owner.

The owner MUST hold the derivation row lock before validating/locking destination publication configuration and MUST supply authoritative transaction-local or persisted output. The helper reuses the same provenance/identity-preserving transaction insertion and does not commit or acquire a second pool connection. It supports atomic missing-publication enqueue inside completion. Two compile-only rustdoc examples prove shared pool and transaction signatures. Existing pool API signature from prior coordination remains unchanged. Owner still owns handler/no-push producer and verified-publication INSERT wiring; verified completed rows must also carry source database and exact ID. Basic shared protocol/helper implemented in cf-protocol/src/{cache,agent}.rs and cf-config/src/cache_credentials.rs. Wire CacheReadAuth::Basic { username, password } strictly requires both string fields and rejects extra/mismatched fields; Debug redacts both. AgentCapabilities.supports_niks3_basic_read is serde-default false, independent of supports_niks3; only owned protocol test/doc initializers updated. No builder write capability or write-helper changes.

Consumer handoff: CF_NETRC_AUTHORITY_SETTING = "cf-netrc-authority"; NixReadFeatures::from_settings_json(&successful_probe.stdout) -> Result<NixReadFeatures>, supports_netrc_authority() -> bool; PreparedCacheRead::new_with_nix_features(url, keys, auth, &features) -> Result<Self>. Legacy new() still supports None/Mtls and rejects Basic before any directory/file creation. Consumers must probe the same runtime executable before preparing the authorized URL/auth snapshot. Prepared fields basic_guard_required: bool and nix_settings: Vec<(String,String)>; apply_to_nix_command(&mut std::process::Command), with Tokio .as_std_mut(), applies all --option entries and child-local CA. Basic settings include require-sigs=true, extra-trusted-public-keys, explicit protected netrc-file and canonical HTTPS origin. Keep the owner alive through termination/reap on every consumer path.

Basic netrc uses one exact normalized host record, always quoted with backslash/quote escaping; no default record. Rejects empty/control-bearing credentials and username colon, insecure/userinfo/fragment/managed-query URL inputs, and absent signing keys. TempDir 0700 and file 0600; credential contents never appear in URL, argv or Debug. Explicit default port 443 normalizes with omitted port; nondefault ports remain in authority. Netrc and guard query overrides are rejected.

Source metrics UI implemented against inspected handlers/api/caches/storage_metrics.rs contract. Owned web-ui api/models.rs now defines CacheMetricsStatus and CacheStorageMetrics with exact status/reason_code/storage_bytes/storage_bytes_basis/object_count/object_count_basis/path_count/measured_at fields. api/client.rs adds GET fetch_cache_storage_metrics with 12-second browser abort budget and cancellation Drop abort, shared authenticated no-store transport, no request body. New private views/caches/metrics.rs loads finite batches of at most three API requests and supplies one shared Display getter to cards/table/details. CachesView now owns a single destination-list resource; after each completed list load it supplies IDs/generation to one metrics resource. Refresh metrics reloads the list/observations and is disabled while a pass is pending. View switches and detail opens reuse the map. Refresh generations hide old measurements immediately; disabled destinations always show Unavailable with explicit disabled explanation, never stale numeric values.
Display uses only available known bases. Logical bytes render GiB (small nonzero totals render <0.01 GiB), with exact byte/basis tooltip and unknown legacy-size exclusion/zero-not-empty/physical-size-not-capacity caveats. Native count renders '<count> objects' under Paths / Objects, never a Nix path count. Both absent fields stay Unavailable. Server UTC observation time is shown in details only for usable evidence. All unavailable/error reasons are static allowlisted explanations; upstream/error/unknown text is never rendered. Removed stale hardcoded Niks3 Storage section in niks3.rs; provider-neutral details now own it. Root Paths cached remains unknown with explanation; no aggregate path inference or capacity ratio. Owner design, backend/protocol/packages/native fixtures and all JS workflows untouched during this metrics scope.

Owner-requested P2 watchdog corrections implemented in the two owned source files. EvaluationWatchdog now uses a bounded Tokio watch channel containing only the monotonic last nonblank line receipt timestamp. Independent runtime within_deadlines monitor enforces idle and immutable spawn-overall deadlines across all collection handler/database/log-flush/final-wait awaits with biased expiry priority. Atomic activity update rejects already-expired and out-of-order receipt timestamps, so buffered output cannot revive expired idle. Removed every collection-side guard.terminate await: collection returns errors/cancellation or is dropped on expiry, then owning scope terminates/reaps on every error/expiry outside all evaluation timers before optional timeout-log persistence. Guard implementation and required existing cleanup tests unchanged. Rustdoc/source invariants now distinguish collection deadlines from untimed explicit cleanup and document cancellation eligibility at two-second loop boundaries, not a wall-clock cancellation bound during a blocked handler. New passing tests: late_or_out_of_order_output_cannot_revive_idle_deadline; expired_idle_precedes_buffered_output_at_601_seconds; outer_idle_expires_during_pending_output_handler_or_log_flush; activity_notifications_rearm_outer_idle_but_not_overall; watchdog_drops_collection_before_cleanup_and_cannot_interrupt_reap (covers both collection error and idle expiry, asserts collection dropped and guard remains armed/owns child before cleanup, crosses overall deadline outside monitor, then proves direct child reaped and descendant killed). Pending atomic completion always rechecks the captured fingerprint, dispatch/session/output/current eligibility and preserves completed historical evidence. Pending retains GC root and log channel. Existing persisted/streamed logs receive only row ID, allowlisted type/stage/outcome and elapsed time under log limits. Pending queue visibility does not guarantee automatic worker recovery.
